| Chi parla | Battuta |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Entra da una porta [[Sir Thomas More]] col [[Lord Mayor|Lord Sindaco]]; da un’altra porta Sir John Munday, ferito |
| [[Lord Mayor|LORD MAYOR]] | Come, Sir John Munday, siete ferito? |
| SIR JOHN | Un piccolo colpo, mio signore. C’era or ora<br><br>una schiera d’apprendisti che giocava di bastone.<br><br>Io comandai loro d’andare alle case dei padroni,<br><br>ma uno di essi, spalleggiato dall’altra masnada,<br><br>mi ferì alla fronte col suo randello;<br><br>e ora, [[Fear|temo]], sono andati a unirsi<br><br>con [[Lincoln]], Sherwin e il loro pericoloso seguito. |
| MORE | I capitani di questa insurrezione<br>hanno preso le armi, e sono venuti or ora<br>a entrambe le prigioni dei Counters, dove hanno liberato<br>diversi prigionieri per debiti, e di là<br>odo che sono andati a San Martino,<br>dove intendono usare violenza<br>agli attoniti lombardi. Perciò, mio signore,<br>se ci sta a cuore la salvezza della [[City|città]],<br>è [[Time|tempo]] che la forza o il parlamentare affronti<br>questi uomini scontenti. |
| *(didascalia)* | Entra un Messaggero |
| [[Lord Mayor|LORD MAYOR]] | Ebbene, che notizie? |
| MESSENGER | Mio signore, i ribelli hanno sfondato Newgate,<br>donde hanno liberato molti prigionieri, zo<br>tanto malfattori quanto notori assassini<br>che disperatamente s’attaccano al loro seguito senza legge. |
| [[Lord Mayor|LORD MAYOR]] | Su il ponte levatoio! Radunate qualche forza<br>a Cornhill e a Cheapside. E, signori,<br>se da ogni parte s’userà diligenza,<br>una quieta bassa marea seguirà questa marea burrascosa. |
| *(didascalia)* | Entrano Shrewsbury, Surrey, Palmer; Cholmley |
| SHREWSBURY | [[Lord Mayor|Lord Sindaco]], sua maestà, ricevuta notizia<br>di questa pericolosissima insurrezione,<br>ha mandato il mio signore di Surrey e me stesso,<br>Sir Thomas Palmer, e i nostri seguaci,<br>ad aggiungere alle vostre forze i nostri migliori mezzi<br>per pacificare questo ammutinamento.<br>In nome di Dio, dunque, muoviamo con felice [[Speed|prestezza]].<br>Il [[King|Re]] si duole se un solo fedele suddito sanguina. |
| SURREY | Odo che intendono dar [[Fire|fuoco]] alle case dei lombardi.<br>O potere, che cosa sei agli occhi d’un pazzo!<br>Fai il tardo idiota sanguinariamente saggio. |
| MORE | Miei signori, non [[Doubt|dubito]] che placheremo<br>con calmo fiato questo flusso di scontento. |
| PALMER | Chiamarli a parlamento senza dubbio<br>può riuscir bene. È [[Well|ben]] detto, messer More. |
| MORE | Andiamo da questi uomini semplici, ché molti sudano<br>sotto quest’atto senza conoscere il debito della legge<br>che pende sulle loro vite. Ché gli sciocchi<br><br>arrancano senza sapere come; come la penna d’un [[Fool|folle]]<br><br>che, finendo, non mostra scritta alcuna sentenza<br><br>legata pur che sia a comune ragione o al più tenue senno.<br><br>Costoro seguono senza intenzione di male, e pure incorrono<br>nella [[Self|stessa]] pena di quelli che levarono questo tumulto.<br>In nome di Dio, avanti, a calmare i nostri nemici privati<br>col fiato della gravità, non con colpi pericolosi. |
| *(didascalia)* | Escono |



<span class="atom-split" data-atom="scenes--scene_6" data-title="Sir Thomas More — Scene 6" data-chapter="Scene 6" data-kind="scene" data-tags="topos/double,archetype/king,archetype/rebel,motif/blood,motif/city,motif/death,motif/desire,motif/grave,motif/horse,motif/love,motif/nature,motif/silence,motif/sleep,motif/tears,motif/well,concept/doubt,concept/faith,concept/fear,concept/honour,concept/mercy,concept/nature,concept/self,setting/court,character/arthur,character/earl_of_surrey,character/lincoln,character/lord_mayor,character/sir_thomas_more"></span>


| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Lincoln]], Doll, Clown [Betts,] George Betts, Williamson, [Sherwin,] others: [Citizens,] [armed] |
| *(didascalia)* | [Addition II (playhouse scribe)]<br>[Addition II (Shakespeare)] |
| [[Lincoln\|LINCOLN]] | Peace, hear me! He that will not see a red herring at a Harry groat, butter at eleven pence a pound, meal at nine shillings a bushel, and beef at four nobles a stone, list to me. |
| OTHER GEORGE BETTS | It will come to that pass if strangers be suffered. Mark him. |
| [[Lincoln\|LINCOLN]] | Our country is a great eating country; argo they eat more in our country than they do in their own. |
| OTHER CLOWN BETTS | By a halfpenny loaf a day, troy weight. |
| [[Lincoln\|LINCOLN]] | They bring in strange roots, which is merely to the undoing of poor prentices. For what’s a sorry parsnip to a good heart? |
| OTHER WILLIAMSON | Trash, trash. They breed sore eyes, and ’tis enough to infect the [[City\|city]] with the palsy. |
| [[Lincoln\|LINCOLN]] | Nay, it has infected it with the palsy, for these bastards of dung—as you know, they grow in dung—have infected us, and it is our infection will make the [[City\|city]] shake. Which partly comes through the eating of parsnips. |
| CLOWN BETTS | True, and pumpkins together. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter a Sergeant-at-arms |
| SERGEANT | What say ye to the [[Mercy\|mercy]] of the [[King]]?<br>Do you refuse it? |
| [[Lincoln\|LINCOLN]] | You would have us upon th’ hip, would you? No, marry, do we not. We accept of the [[King]]’s [[Mercy\|mercy]], but we will show no [[Mercy\|mercy]] upon the strangers. |
| SERGEANT | You are the simplest things that ever stood In such a question. |
| [[Lincoln\|LINCOLN]] | How say you now, prentices? Prentices ‘simple’? Down with him! |
| ALL CITIZENS | Prentices simple? Prentices simple? |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the [[Lord Mayor]], Surrey, Shrewsbury, [More, Palmer] |
| MAYOR | Hold, in the [[King]]’s name, hold! |
| SURREY | Friends, masters, countrymen— |
| [[Lord Mayor\|LORD MAYOR]] | Peace ho, peace! I charge you keep the peace. |
| SHREWSBURY | My masters, countrymen— |
| WILLIAMSON | The noble Earl of Shrewsbury! Let’s hear him. |
| GEORGE BETTS | We’ll hear the [[Earl of Surrey]]. |
| [[Lincoln\|LINCOLN]] | The Earl of Shrewsbury! |
| GEORGE BEETS | We’ll hear both. |
| ALL CITIZENS | Both, both, both, both! |
| [[Lincoln\|LINCOLN]] | Peace, I say, peace! Are you men of wisdom, or what are you? |
| SURREY | What you will have them, but not men of wisdom. |
| SOME CITIZENS | We’ll not hear my lord of Surrey. |
| OTHER CITIZENS | No, no, no, no, no! Shrewsbury, Shrewsbury! |
| MORE | Whiles they are o’er the bank of their obedience<br>Thus will they bear down all things. |
| [[Lincoln\|LINCOLN]] | Sheriff More speaks. Shall we hear Sheriff More speak? |
| DOLL | Let’s hear him. A keeps a plentiful shrievaltry, and a made my brother, [[arthur\|Arthur]] Watchins, Sergeant Safe’s yeoman. Let’s hear Sheriff More! |
| ALL CITIZENS | Sheriff More, More, More, Sheriff More! |
| MORE | Even by the rule you have among yourselves,<br>Command still audience. |
| SOME CITIZENS | Surrey, Surrey! |
| OTHER CITIZENS | More, More! |
| [[Lincoln\|LINCOLN]] | and GEORGE BETTS Peace, peace, [[Silence\|silence]], peace! |
| MORE | You that have voice and credit with the number,<br>Command them to a stillness. |
| [[Lincoln\|LINCOLN]] | A plague on them, they will not hold their peace. The devil cannot rule them. |
| MORE | Then what a rough and riotous charge have you<br>To lead those that the devil cannot rule.—<br>Good masters, hear me speak. |
| DOLL | Ay, by th’ mass will we, More. Thou’rt a good housekeeper, and I thank thy good worship for my brother [[arthur\|Arthur]] Watchins. |
| ALL THE OTHER CITIZENS | Peace, peace! |
| MORE | Look what you do offend you cry upon;<br>That is, the peace. Not one of you here present,<br>Had there such fellows lived when you were babes<br>That could have topped the peace as now you would,<br>The peace wherein you have till now grown up<br>Had been ta’en from you, and the bloody times<br>Could not have brought you to the state of men.<br>Alas, poor things! What is it you have got<br>Although we grant you get the thing you seek? |
| GEORGE BETTS | Marry, the removing of the strangers, which cannot choose but much advantage the poor handicrafts of the [[City\|city]]. |
| MORE | Grant them removed, and grant that this your noise<br>Hath chid down all the majesty of England.<br>Imagine that you see the wretched strangers,<br>Their babies at their backs, with their poor luggage,<br>Plodding to th’ ports and coasts for transportation,<br>And that you sit as kings in your desires,<br>Authority quite silenced by your brawl,<br>And you in ruff of your opinions clothed:<br>What had you got? I’ll tell you: you had taught<br>How insolence and strong hand should prevail,<br>How order should be quelled. And by this pattern<br>Not one of you should live an aged man;<br>For other ruffians, as their fancies wrought,<br>With selfsame hand, [[Self\|self]] reasons, and [[Self\|self]] right,<br>Would shark on you, and men, like ravenous fishes,<br>Would feed on one another. |
| DOLL | Before God, that’s as true as the gospel. |
| [[Lincoln\|LINCOLN]] | Nay, this’ a sound fellow, I tell you. Let’s mark him. |
| MORE | Let me set up before your thoughts, good friends,<br>One supposition, which if you will mark<br>You shall perceive how horrible a shape<br>Your innovation bears. First, ‘tis a sin<br>Which oft th’apostle did forewarn us of,<br>Urging obedience to authority;<br>And ‘twere no error if I told you all<br>You were in arms ’gainst God. |
| ALL CITIZENS | Marry, God forbid thatl |
| MORE | Nay, certainly you are. no<br>For to the [[King\|king]] God hath His office lent<br>Of dread, of justice, power, and command;<br>Hath bid him rule, and willed you to obey.<br>And, to add ampler majesty to this,<br>He hath not only lent the [[King\|king]] His figure,<br>His throne and [[Sword\|sword]], but given him His own name:<br>Calls him a god on earth. What do you, then,<br>Rising ‘gainst him that God Himself installs,<br>But rise ’gainst God? What do you to your souls<br>In doing this? O, desperate as you are,<br>Wash your foul minds with [[Tears\|tears]], and those same hands<br>That you, like rebels, lift against the peace,<br>Lift up for peace; and your unreverent knees,<br>Make them your feet. To kneel to be forgiven<br>Is safer wars than ever you can make<br><br>Whose discipline is riot.<br><br>In, in, to your obedience! Why, even your hurly<br><br>Cannot proceed but by obedience.<br><br>Tell me but this: What [[Rebel\|rebel]] captain,<br>As mutinies are incident, by his name<br>Can still the rout? Who will obey a traitor?<br>Or how can [[Well\|well]] that proclamation sound<br>When there is no addition but ‘a [[Rebel\|rebel]]’<br>To qualify a [[Rebel\|rebel]]? You’ll put down strangers,<br>Kill them, cut their throats, possess their houses,<br>And lead the majesty of law in lyam<br>To slip him like a hound. Alas, alas! Say now the [[King]],<br>As he is clement if th’offender mourn,<br>Should so much come too short of your great trespass<br>As but to banish you: whither would you go?<br>What country, by the [[Nature\|nature]] of your error,<br>Should give you harbour? Go you to France or<br>Flanders,<br>To any German province, Spain or Portugal,<br>Nay, anywhere that not adheres to England:<br>Why, you must needs be strangers. Would you be<br>pleased<br>To find a nation of such barbarous temper<br>That, breaking out in hideous violence,<br>Would not afford you an abode on earth,<br>Whet their detested knives against your throats,<br>Spurn you like [[dogs]], and like as if that God<br>Owed not nor made not you, nor that the elements<br>Were not all appropriate to your comforts,<br>But chartered unto them? What would you think<br>To be thus used? This is the strangers’ case,<br>And this your mountainish inhumanity. |
| ALL CITIZENS | [[Faith]], a says true. Let us do as we may be done by. |
| ALL CITIZENS [[Lincoln\|LINCOLN]] | We’ll be ruled by you, Master More, if you’ll stand our friend to procure our pardon. |
| MORE | Submit you to these noble gentlemen,<br>Entreat their mediation to the [[King]],<br>Give up yourself to form, obey the magistrate,<br>And there’s no [[Doubt\|doubt]] but [[Mercy\|mercy]] may be found<br>If you so seek it.<br><br>[Addition 11 (Shakespeare)]<br>[Original Text (Munday)] |
| ALL CITIZENS | We yield, and [[Desire\|desire]] his highness’ [[Mercy\|mercy]]. They lay by their weapons |
| MORE | No [[Doubt\|doubt]] his majesty will grant it you.<br>But you must yield to go to several prisons<br>Till that his highness’ will be further known. |
| ALL CITIZENS | Most willingly, whither you will have us. |
| SHREWSBURY | [[Lord Mayor]], let them be sent to several prisons,<br>And there, in any case, be [[Well\|well]] entreated.<br>My lord of Surrey, please you to take [[Horse\|horse]]<br>And ride to Cheapside, where the aldermen<br>Are with their several companies in arms.<br>Will them to go unto their several wards,<br>Both for the stay of further mutiny<br>And for the apprehending of such persons<br>As shall contend. |
| SURREY | I go, my noble lord. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit |
| SHREWSBURY | We’ll straight go tell his highness these good news.<br>Withal, Sheriff More, I’ll tell him how your breath<br>Hath ransomed many a subject from sad [[Death\|death]]. Exit |
| [[Lord Mayor\|LORD MAYOR]] | [[Lincoln]] and Sherwin, you shall both to Newgate,<br>The rest unto the Counters. |
| PALMER | Go, guard them hence. A little breath [[Well\|well]] spent<br>Cheats expectation in his fair’st event. |
| DOLL | [[Well]], Sheriff More, thou hast done more with thy good words than all they could with their weapons. Give me thy hand. Keep thy promise now for the [[King]]’s pardon, or, by the Lord, I’ll call thee a plain cony-catcher. |
| [[Lincoln\|LINCOLN]] | Farewell, Sheriff More. And as we yield by thee<br>So make our peace; then thou deal’st honestly. |
| CLOWN BETTS | Ay, and save us from the gallows, else a deals [[Double\|double]] honestly.<br>[The Citizens] are led away |
| [[Lord Mayor\|LORD MAYOR]] | Master Sheriff More, you have preserved the [[City\|city]]<br>From a most dangerous fierce commotion.<br>For if this limb of riot here in St Martin’s<br>Had joined with other branches of the [[City\|city]]<br>That did begin to kindle, ’twould have bred<br>Great rage. That rage much murder would have fed. |
| PALMER | Not steel but eloquence hath wrought this good.<br><br>You have redeemed us from much threatened [[Blood\|blood]]. |
| MORE | My lord, and brethren, what I here have spoke<br>My country’s [[Love\|love]] and, next, the [[City\|city]]’s care<br>Enjoined me to; which since it thus prevails,<br>Think God hath made weak More His instrument<br>To thwart sedition’s violent intent.<br>I think ’twere best, my lord, some two hours hence<br>We meet at the Guildhall, and there determine<br>That thorough every ward the watch be clad<br>In armour. But especially provide<br>That at the [[City\|city]] gates selected men,<br>Substantial citizens, do ward tonight,<br>For [[Fear\|fear]] of further mischief. |
| [[Lord Mayor\|LORD MAYOR]] | It shall be so. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Shrewsbury |
| [[Lord Mayor\|LORD MAYOR]] | But yon, methinks, my lord of Shrewsbury. |
| SHREWSBURY | My lord, his majesty sends loving thanks<br>To you, your brethren, and his faithful subjects<br>Your careful citizens. But Master More, to you<br>A rougher yet as kind a salutation.<br>Your name is yet too short. Nay, you must kneel.<br>A knight’s creation is this knightly steel. |
| *(didascalia)* | More kneels |
| SHREWSBURY | Rise up [[Sir Thomas More]]. |
| *(didascalia)* | He knights More |
| *(didascalia)* | MORE rising |
| SHREWSBURY | I thank his highness for thus honouring me. |
| SHREWSBURY | This is but first taste of his princely favour,<br>For it hath pleased his high majesty,<br>Noting your wisdom and deserving merit,<br>To put this staff of [[Honour\|honour]] in your hand,<br>For he hath chose you of his Privy Council. |
| *(didascalia)* | He gives More a staff of office |
| MORE | My lord, for to deny my sovereign’s bounty<br>Were to drop precious stones into the heaps<br>Whence first they came.<br>To urge my imperfections in excuse<br>Were all as stale as custom. No, my lord,<br>My service is my [[King\|king]]’s. Good reason why,<br>Since life or [[Death\|death]] hangs on our sovereign’s eye. |
| [[Lord Mayor\|LORD MAYOR]] | His majesty hath honoured much the [[City\|city]]<br>In this his princely choice. |
| MORE | My lord and brethren,<br>Though I depart for [[Court\|court]], my [[Love\|love]] shall rest<br>〈 〉<br>I now must [[Sleep\|sleep]] in [[Court\|court]], sound sleeps forbear.<br>The chamberlain to state is public care.<br>Yet in this rising of my private [[Blood\|blood]]<br>My studious thoughts shall tend the [[City\|city]]’s good. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Croft |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Crofts |
| SHREWSBURY | How now, Crofts? What news? |
| CROFTS | My lord, his highness sends express command<br>That a record be entered of this riot,<br>And that the chief and capital offenders<br>Be thereon straight arraigned; for himself intends<br>To sit in person on the rest tomorrow<br>At Westminster. |
| SHREWSBURY | [[Lord Mayor]], you hear your charge.<br>Come, good [[Sir Thomas More]], to [[Court\|court]] let’s hie.<br>You are th’appeaser of this mutiny. |
| MORE | My lord, farewell. New days begets new tides.<br>Life whirls ’bout fate, then to a [[Grave\|grave]] it slides. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt severally |


<span class="qlang-split" data-lang="it"></span>

| Chi parla | Battuta |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Entrano [[Lincoln]], Doll, il Buffone [Betts,] George Betts, Williamson, [Sherwin,] e altri: [Cittadini,] [armati] |
| *(didascalia)* | [Aggiunta II (copista del teatro)]<br>[Aggiunta II (Shakespeare)] |
| [[Lincoln|LINCOLN]] | Silenzio, ascoltatemi! Chi non vuol vedere un’aringa affumicata a un groat di Enrico, il burro a undici pence la libbra, la farina a nove scellini lo staio, e il manzo a quattro nobili la pietra, dia ascolto a me. |
| OTHER GEORGE BETTS | Si arriverà a quel punto se si tollerano gli stranieri. Ascoltatelo. |
| [[Lincoln|LINCOLN]] | Il nostro paese è un gran paese mangiatore; ergo essi mangiano più nel nostro paese di quanto facciano nel loro. |
| OTHER CLOWN BETTS | Di una pagnotta da mezzo penny al giorno, a peso di troy. |
| [[Lincoln|LINCOLN]] | Portano dentro radici strane, il che serve unicamente alla rovina dei poveri apprendisti. Ché cos’è una misera pastinaca per un cuore gagliardo? |
| OTHER WILLIAMSON | Robaccia, robaccia. Fanno venire il male agli occhi, e basta a infettare la [[City|città]] di paralisia. |
| [[Lincoln|LINCOLN]] | Anzi, l’ha già infettata di paralisia, ché questi bastardi del letame—come sapete, crescono nel letame—hanno infettato noi, e sarà la nostra infezione a far tremare la [[City|città]]. Il che viene in parte dal mangiar pastinache. |
| CLOWN BETTS | Vero, e zucche insieme. |
| *(didascalia)* | Entra un Sergente d’arme |
| SERGEANT | Che dite alla [[Mercy|clemenza]] del [[King|Re]]?<br>La rifiutate? |
| [[Lincoln|LINCOLN]] | Vorreste averci in pugno, eh? No, perbacco, no davvero. Accettiamo la [[Mercy|clemenza]] del [[King|Re]], ma non useremo alcuna [[Mercy|clemenza]] verso gli stranieri. |
| SERGEANT | Siete le creature più semplici che mai si trovarono in tal questione. |
| [[Lincoln|LINCOLN]] | Che ne dite ora, apprendisti? Apprendisti «semplici»? Abbasso lui! |
| ALL CITIZENS | Apprendisti semplici? Apprendisti semplici? |
| *(didascalia)* | Entrano il [[Lord Mayor]], Surrey, Shrewsbury, [More, Palmer] |
| MAYOR | Fermi, in nome del [[King|Re]], fermi! |
| SURREY | Amici, padroni, compatrioti— |
| [[Lord Mayor|LORD MAYOR]] | Pace, olà, pace! Vi ingiungo di mantenere la pace. |
| SHREWSBURY | Miei padroni, compatrioti— |
| WILLIAMSON | Il nobile conte di Shrewsbury! Ascoltiamolo. |
| GEORGE BETTS | Ascolteremo il [[Earl of Surrey|conte di Surrey]]. |
| [[Lincoln|LINCOLN]] | Il conte di Shrewsbury! |
| GEORGE BEETS | Ascolteremo entrambi. |
| ALL CITIZENS | Entrambi, entrambi, entrambi, entrambi! |
| [[Lincoln|LINCOLN]] | Pace, dico, pace! Siete uomini di senno, o che siete? |
| SURREY | Quel che vorrete farne, ma non uomini di senno. |
| SOME CITIZENS | Non ascolteremo il mio signore di Surrey. |
| OTHER CITIZENS | No, no, no, no, no! Shrewsbury, Shrewsbury! |
| MORE | Finché sono oltre l’argine della loro obbedienza<br>così travolgeranno ogni cosa. |
| [[Lincoln|LINCOLN]] | Parla lo sceriffo More. Vogliamo udir parlare lo sceriffo More? |
| DOLL | Ascoltiamolo. Tiene uno sceriffato abbondante, e fece di mio fratello, [[arthur|Arthur]] Watchins, il famiglio del sergente Safe. Ascoltiamo lo sceriffo More! |
| ALL CITIZENS | Sceriffo More, More, More, sceriffo More! |
| MORE | Per la regola stessa che avete fra voi,<br>imponete ancora udienza. |
| SOME CITIZENS | Surrey, Surrey! |
| OTHER CITIZENS | More, More! |
| [[Lincoln|LINCOLN]] | e GEORGE BETTS Pace, pace, [[Silence|silenzio]], pace! |
| MORE | Voi che avete voce e credito presso la moltitudine,<br>imponete loro la quiete. |
| [[Lincoln|LINCOLN]] | La peste li colga, non vogliono tacere. Il diavolo non li può governare. |
| MORE | Che rude e tumultuosa carica avete dunque,<br>guidare coloro che il diavolo non può governare.—<br>Buoni padroni, uditemi parlare. |
| DOLL | Sì, per la messa, ti udiremo, More. Sei un buon padrone di casa, e ringrazio la tua buona signoria per mio fratello [[arthur|Arthur]] Watchins. |
| ALL THE OTHER CITIZENS | Pace, pace! |
| MORE | Guardate: ciò che offendete è ciò che invocate;<br>e cioè la pace. Non uno di voi qui presente,<br>fossero vissuti simili compagni quando eravate infanti<br>che avessero potuto troncare la pace come ora vorreste voi,<br>la pace nella quale finora siete cresciuti<br>vi sarebbe stata tolta, e i tempi sanguinosi<br>non v’avrebbero condotti allo stato d’uomini.<br>Ahimè, poveri esseri! Che cos’è che avete ottenuto,<br>anche concedendovi che otteniate ciò che cercate? |
| GEORGE BETTS | Perbacco, l’allontanamento degli stranieri, il che non può non giovare assai ai poveri artigiani della [[City|città]]. |
| MORE | Concedete che siano allontanati, e concedete che questo vostro clamore<br>abbia zittito tutta la maestà d’Inghilterra.<br>Immaginate di vedere i miseri stranieri,<br>i loro bambini in spalla, coi loro poveri fardelli,<br>arrancare verso i porti e le coste per l’imbarco,<br>e voi seduti come re nei vostri desideri,<br>l’autorità del tutto ammutolita dal vostro tumulto,<br>e voi vestiti della gorgiera delle vostre opinioni:<br>che cosa avreste ottenuto? Ve lo dirò io: avreste insegnato<br>come l’insolenza e la mano forte debbano prevalere,<br>come l’ordine debba essere soffocato. E secondo questo modello<br>non uno di voi giungerebbe a vecchiaia;<br>ché altri manigoldi, come i loro capricci suggerissero,<br>con la medesima mano, le [[Self|stesse]] ragioni e lo [[Self|stesso]] diritto,<br>vi prederebbero, e gli uomini, come pesci voraci,<br>si nutrirebbero l’uno dell’altro. |
| DOLL | Davanti a Dio, questo è vero come il vangelo. |
| [[Lincoln|LINCOLN]] | Anzi, costui è un uomo assennato, ve lo dico io. Stiamo a sentirlo. |
| MORE | Lasciate ch’io ponga davanti ai vostri pensieri, buoni amici,<br>una sola supposizione, la quale, se vorrete considerarla,<br>vi farà vedere quale orribile forma<br>abbia la vostra innovazione. Primo, è un peccato<br>del quale spesso l’apostolo ci ammonì,<br>esortando all’obbedienza verso l’autorità;<br>e non sarebbe errore se vi dicessi a tutti<br>che siete in armi contro Dio. |
| ALL CITIZENS | Perbacco, Dio non voglia questo! |
| MORE | Anzi, certamente lo siete. no<br>Ché al [[King|re]] Dio ha prestato il Suo ufficio<br>di terrore, di giustizia, potere e comando;<br>gli ha ordinato di regnare, e ha voluto che voi obbediate.<br>E, per aggiungere a ciò più ampia maestà,<br>non solo ha prestato al [[King|re]] la Sua figura,<br>il Suo trono e la Sua [[Sword|spada]], ma gli ha dato il Suo proprio nome:<br>lo chiama un dio in terra. Che fate dunque,<br>levandovi contro colui che Dio stesso insedia,<br>se non levarvi contro Dio? Che fate alle vostre anime<br>facendo questo? O disperati che siete,<br>lavate le vostre menti immonde con le [[Tears|lacrime]], e quelle stesse mani<br>che voi, da ribelli, levate contro la pace,<br>levatele per la pace; e le vostre ginocchia irriverenti,<br>fatene i vostri piedi. Inginocchiarsi per esser perdonati<br>è guerra più sicura di quante mai possiate farne<br><br>voi la cui disciplina è il tumulto.<br><br>Dentro, dentro, alla vostra obbedienza! Ma se persino il vostro trambusto<br><br>non può procedere se non per obbedienza.<br><br>Ditemi solo questo: quale capitano [[Rebel|ribelle]],<br>come accade negli ammutinamenti, col suo nome<br>può quietare la turba? Chi obbedirà a un traditore?<br>O come può suonare [[Well|bene]] quel bando<br>quando non v’è altro titolo che «un [[Rebel|ribelle]]»<br>per qualificare un [[Rebel|ribelle]]? Volete abbattere gli stranieri,<br>ucciderli, sgozzarli, impadronirvi delle loro case,<br>e menare al guinzaglio la maestà della legge<br>per scioglierla come un segugio. Ahimè, ahimè! Poniamo ora che il [[King|Re]],<br>com’è clemente se l’offensore si duole,<br>restasse tanto al di sotto della vostra grande colpa<br>da solo bandirvi: dove andreste?<br>Quale paese, per la [[Nature|natura]] del vostro errore,<br>dovrebbe darvi rifugio? Andate in Francia o nelle<br>Fiandre,<br>in qualsiasi provincia tedesca, in Spagna o in Portogallo,<br>anzi, ovunque non aderisca all’Inghilterra:<br>ebbene, sareste per forza stranieri. Vi sarebbe<br>grato<br>trovare una nazione di tempra così barbara<br>che, scoppiando in orrenda violenza,<br>non vi concedesse una dimora sulla terra,<br>affilasse i suoi detestati coltelli contro le vostre gole,<br>vi cacciasse come [[dogs|cani]], e come se Dio<br>non vi possedesse né v’avesse fatti, né gli elementi<br>fossero destinati ai vostri conforti,<br>ma concessi in privilegio a loro? Che ne direste<br>d’essere trattati così? Questo è il caso degli stranieri,<br>e questa la vostra montanara disumanità. |
| ALL CITIZENS | In [[Faith|fede]], dice il vero. Facciamo agli altri come vorremmo fosse fatto a noi. |
| ALL CITIZENS [[Lincoln|LINCOLN]] | Ci lasceremo governare da voi, messer More, se vorrete esserci amico per procurarci il perdono. |
| MORE | Sottomettetevi a questi nobili gentiluomini,<br>implorate la loro mediazione presso il [[King|Re]],<br>arrendetevi alla legge, obbedite al magistrato,<br>e non v’è [[Doubt|dubbio]] che [[Mercy|clemenza]] si possa trovare<br>se così la cercate.<br><br>[Aggiunta 11 (Shakespeare)]<br>[Testo originale (Munday)] |
| ALL CITIZENS | Cediamo, e [[Desire|desideriamo]] la [[Mercy|clemenza]] di sua altezza. Depongono le armi |
| MORE | Non [[Doubt|dubito]] che sua maestà ve la concederà.<br>Ma dovete acconsentire ad andare in diverse prigioni<br>finché la volontà di sua altezza non sia meglio nota. |
| ALL CITIZENS | Ben volentieri, dovunque vorrete condurci. |
| SHREWSBURY | [[Lord Mayor|Lord Sindaco]], siano mandati in diverse prigioni,<br>e là, in ogni caso, siano [[Well|ben]] trattati.<br>Mio signore di Surrey, piacciavi prendere il [[Horse|cavallo]]<br>e cavalcare a Cheapside, dove gli aldermanni<br>stanno in armi con le loro diverse compagnie.<br>Ordinate loro d’andare ai rispettivi quartieri,<br>tanto per impedire ulteriore ammutinamento<br>quanto per l’arresto di quelle persone<br>che facessero resistenza. |
| SURREY | Vado, mio nobile signore. |
| *(didascalia)* | Esce |
| SHREWSBURY | Andremo subito a recare a sua altezza queste buone nuove.<br>E per giunta, sceriffo More, gli dirò come il vostro fiato<br>abbia riscattato più d’un suddito da triste [[Death|morte]]. Esce |
| [[Lord Mayor|LORD MAYOR]] | [[Lincoln]] e Sherwin, andrete entrambi a Newgate,<br>gli altri ai Counters. |
| PALMER | Andate, scortateli via di qui. Un poco di fiato [[Well|ben]] speso<br>inganna l’aspettativa nel suo più bell’esito. |
| DOLL | [[Well|Bene]], sceriffo More, hai fatto più tu con le tue buone parole di quanto potessero tutti loro con le armi. Dammi la mano. Mantieni ora la promessa del perdono del [[King|Re]], o, per il Signore, ti chiamerò un bel truffatore. |
| [[Lincoln|LINCOLN]] | Addio, sceriffo More. E come per te cediamo,<br>così fa’ la nostra pace; allora agisci onestamente. |
| CLOWN BETTS | Sì, e salvaci dalla forca, altrimenti agisce [[Double|doppiamente]] onesto.<br>[I Cittadini] sono condotti via |
| [[Lord Mayor|LORD MAYOR]] | Messer sceriffo More, avete preservato la [[City|città]]<br>da un tumulto pericolosissimo e feroce.<br>Ché se questo ramo di sommossa qui a San Martino<br>si fosse unito ad altri rami della [[City|città]]<br>che cominciavano a prender fuoco, avrebbe generato<br>gran furore. E quel furore avrebbe nutrito molte uccisioni. |
| PALMER | Non l’acciaio ma l’eloquenza ha operato questo bene.<br><br>Ci avete redenti da molto minacciato [[Blood|sangue]]. |
| MORE | Mio signore, e fratelli, ciò che qui ho detto<br>l’[[Love|amore]] del mio paese e, poi, la cura della [[City|città]]<br>me l’imposero; e poiché così prevale,<br>pensate che Dio abbia fatto del debole More il Suo strumento<br>per sventare il violento intento della sedizione.<br>Penso sarebbe meglio, mio signore, che fra due ore<br>c’incontrassimo alla Guildhall, e là stabilissimo<br>che per ogni quartiere la guardia sia vestita<br>d’armatura. Ma soprattutto provvedete<br>che alle porte della [[City|città]] uomini scelti,<br>cittadini di sostanza, montino la guardia stanotte,<br>per [[Fear|timore]] d’altri danni. |
| [[Lord Mayor|LORD MAYOR]] | Così sarà. |
| *(didascalia)* | Entra Shrewsbury |
| [[Lord Mayor|LORD MAYOR]] | Ma laggiù, mi pare, il mio signore di Shrewsbury. |
| SHREWSBURY | Mio signore, sua maestà manda amorevoli ringraziamenti<br>a voi, ai vostri fratelli, e ai suoi fedeli sudditi,<br>i vostri solleciti cittadini. Ma a voi, messer More,<br>un saluto più ruvido e pur altrettanto benigno.<br>Il vostro nome è ancora troppo corto. Anzi, dovete inginocchiarvi.<br>Creazione d’un cavaliere è questo cavalleresco acciaio. |
| *(didascalia)* | More s’inginocchia |
| SHREWSBURY | Alzatevi, [[Sir Thomas More]]. |
| *(didascalia)* | Lo fa cavaliere |
| *(didascalia)* | MORE alzandosi |
| SHREWSBURY | Ringrazio sua altezza d’avermi così onorato. |
| SHREWSBURY | Questo è solo il primo saggio del suo principesco favore,<br>ché è piaciuto alla sua alta maestà,<br>notando la vostra saggezza e il vostro merito degno,<br>di porvi in mano questo bastone d’[[Honour|onore]],<br>ché v’ha scelto nel suo Consiglio Privato. |
| *(didascalia)* | Dà a More un bastone di carica |
| MORE | Mio signore, negare la munificenza del mio sovrano<br>sarebbe come lasciar cadere pietre preziose nei mucchi<br>donde prima vennero.<br>Addurre le mie imperfezioni per scusa<br>sarebbe stantio quanto l’usanza. No, mio signore,<br>il mio servizio è del mio [[King|re]]. E con buona ragione,<br>poiché vita o [[Death|morte]] pende dall’occhio del nostro sovrano. |
| [[Lord Mayor|LORD MAYOR]] | Sua maestà ha molto onorato la [[City|città]]<br>in questa sua principesca scelta. |
| MORE | Mio signore e fratelli,<br>benché io parta per la [[Court|corte]], il mio [[Love|amore]] resterà<br>〈 〉<br>Ora devo [[Sleep|dormire]] a [[Court|corte]]: sonni profondi, astenetevi.<br>Il ciambellano dello Stato è la cura pubblica.<br>Pure, in questo salire del mio [[Blood|sangue]] privato<br>i miei studiosi pensieri veglieranno al bene della [[City|città]]. |
| *(didascalia)* | Entra Croft |
| *(didascalia)* | Entra Crofts |
| SHREWSBURY | Ebbene, Crofts? Che notizie? |
| CROFTS | Mio signore, sua altezza manda espresso comando<br>che sia steso verbale di questo tumulto,<br>e che i principali e capitali offensori<br>siano per esso subito processati; ché egli stesso intende<br>giudicare in persona gli altri domani<br>a Westminster. |
| SHREWSBURY | [[Lord Mayor|Lord Sindaco]], udite il vostro incarico.<br>Venite, buon [[Sir Thomas More]], affrettiamoci a [[Court|corte]].<br>Voi siete il pacificatore di questo ammutinamento. |
| MORE | Mio signore, addio. Nuovi giorni generano nuove maree.<br>La vita turbina intorno al fato, poi scivola in una [[Grave|tomba]]. |
| *(didascalia)* | Escono separatamente |



<span class="atom-split" data-atom="scenes--scene_7" data-title="Sir Thomas More — Scene 7" data-chapter="Scene 7" data-kind="scene" data-tags="archetype/king,motif/blood,motif/city,motif/death,motif/desire,motif/journey,motif/love,motif/pain,motif/time,motif/well,concept/faith,concept/hope,concept/mercy,setting/london,character/lincoln,character/nurse,character/sir_thomas_more"></span>


| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Master Sheriff, and meet a Messenger |
| SHERIFF | Messenger, what news? |
| MESSENGER | Is execution yet performed? |
| SHERIFF | Not yet. The carts stand ready at the stairs,<br>And they shall presently away to Tyburn. |
| MESSENGER | Stay, Master Sheriff. It is the Council’s pleasure,<br>For more example in so bad a case,<br>A gibbet be erected in Cheapside<br>Hard by the Standard, whither you must bring<br>[[Lincoln]], and those that were the chief with him,<br>To suffer [[Death\|death]], and that immediately. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Officers |
| SHERIFF | It shall be done, sir. Exit Messenger<br>Officers, be speedy.<br>Call for a gibbet, see it be erected.<br>Others make haste to Newgate; bid them bring<br>The prisoners hither, for they here must die.<br>Away, I say, and see no [[Time\|time]] be slacked. |
| OFFICERS | We go, sir. |
| SHERIFF | That’s [[Well\|well]] said, fellows. Now you do your duty. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt some Officers severally. Others set up the gibbet |
| SHERIFF | God for his pity help these troublous times!<br><br>The street’s stopped up with gazing multitudes.<br><br>Command our armed officers with halberds<br><br>Make way for entrance of the prisoners.<br><br>Let proclamation once again be made<br><br>That every householder, on [[Pain\|pain]] of [[Death\|death]],<br><br>Keep in his prentices, and every man<br><br>Stand with a weapon ready at his door,<br><br>As he will answer to the contrary. |
| FIRST OFFICER | I’ll see it done, sir. Exit |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter another Offices |
| SHERIFF | Bring them away to execution.<br><br>The writ is come above two hours since.<br><br>The [[City\|city]] will be fined for this neglect. |
| SECOND OFFICER | There’s such a press and multitude at Newgate<br>They cannot bring the carts unto the stairs<br>To take the prisoners in. |
| SHERIFF | Then let them come on foot.<br>We may not dally [[Time\|time]] with great command. |
| SECOND OFFICER | Some of the Bench, sir, think it very fit<br>That stay be made, and give it out abroad<br>The execution is deferred till morning;<br>And when the streets shall be a little cleared<br>To chain them up, and suddenly dispatch it. |
| *(didascalia)* | The prisoners are brought in, amongst them [[Lincoln]], |
| *(didascalia)* | Doll, Williamson, Clown Betts, and Sherwin, [[Well\|well]] |
| *(didascalia)* | guarded, and the Executioner |
| SHERIFF | Stay, in meantime methinks they come along.<br>See, they are coming. So, ’tis very [[Well\|well]].<br>Bring [[Lincoln]] there the first unto the tree. |
| CLOWN BETTS | Ay, for I cry lag, sir. |
| [[Lincoln\|LINCOLN]] | I knew the first, sir, did belong to me.<br>This the old proverb now complete doth make:<br>That ‘[[Lincoln]] should be hanged for [[London]]’s sake’. |
| *(didascalia)* | I’ God’s name, let’s to work. To Executioner Fellow, dispatch. |
| [[Lincoln\|LINCOLN]] | He goes up<br>I was the foremost man in this rebellion,<br>And I the foremost that must die for it. |
| DOLL | Bravely, John [[Lincoln]], let thy [[Death\|death]] express<br>That, as thou lived‘st a man, thou died’st no less. |
| [[Lincoln\|LINCOLN]] | Doll Williamson, thine eyes shall witness it.<br>Then to all you that come to view mine end<br>I must confess I had no ill intent<br>But against such as wronged us overmuch.<br>And now I can perceive it was not fit<br>That private men should carve out their redress<br>Which way they list. No, learn it now by me:<br>Obedience is the best in each degree.<br>And, asking [[Mercy\|mercy]] meekly of my [[King\|king]],<br>I patiently submit me to the law.<br>But God forgive them that were cause of it;<br>And, as a Christian, truly from my heart,<br>I likewise crave they would forgive me too,<br>〈 〉<br>That others by example of the same<br>Henceforth be warned to attempt the like<br>’Gainst any alien that repaireth hither.<br>Fare ye [[Well\|well]] all. The next [[Time\|time]] that we meet<br>I trust in heaven we shall each other greet. He leaps off |
| DOLL | Farewell, John [[Lincoln]]. Say all what they can,<br>Thou lived‘st a good fellow, and died’st an honest man. |
| CLOWN BETTS | Would I were so far on my [[Journey\|journey]]. The first stretch is the worst, methinks. |
| SHERIFF | Bring Williamson there forward. |
| DOLL | Good Master Sheriff, I have an earnest suit,<br>And, as you are a man, deny’t me not. |
| SHERIFF | Woman, what is it? Be it in my power,<br>Thou shalt obtain it. |
| DOLL | Let me die next, sir, that is all I crave.<br>You know not what a comfort you shall bring<br>To my poor heart to die before my husband. |
| SHERIFF | Bring her to [[Death\|death]]. She shall have her [[Desire\|desire]]. |
| CLOWN BETTS | Sir, and I have a suit to you too. |
| SHERIFF | What is it? |
| CLOWN BETTS | That, as you have hanged [[Lincoln]] first and will hang her next, so you will not hang me at all. |
| SHERIFF | Nay, you set ope the Counter gates, and you must hang chiefly. |
| CLOWN BETTS | [[Well]] then, so much for that! |
| *(didascalia)* | DOLL to Sherif |
| CLOWN BETTS | Sir, your free bounty much contents my mind.<br>Commend me to that good sheriff Master More,<br>And tell him had’t not been for his [[Persuasion\|persuasion]]<br>John [[Lincoln]] had not hung here as he does.<br>We would first have locked up in Leaden Hall,<br>And there been burned to ashes with the roof. |
| SHERIFF | Woman, what Master More did was a subject’s duty,<br>And hath so pleased our gracious lord the [[King]]<br>That he is hence removed to higher place<br>And made of Council to his majesty. |
| DOLL | [[Well]] is he worthy of it, by my troth:<br>An honest, wise, [[Well\|well]]-spoken gentleman.<br>Yet would I praise his honesty much more<br>If he had kept his word and saved our lives.<br>But let that pass. Men are but men, and so<br>Words are but words, and pays not what men owe.<br>Now, husband, since perhaps the world may say<br>That through my means thou com’st thus to thy end,<br>Here I begin this cup of [[Death\|death]] to thee,<br>Because thou shalt be sure to taste no worse<br>Than I have taken that must go before thee.<br>What though I be a woman? That’s no matter.<br>I do owe God a [[Death\|death]], and I must pay him.<br>Husband, give me thy hand. Be not dismayed.<br>This chore being chored, then all our debt is paid.<br>Only two little babes we leave behind us,<br>And all I can bequeath them at this [[Time\|time]]<br>Is but the [[Love\|love]] of some good honest friend<br>To bring them up in charitable sort.<br>What, masters?—He goes upright that never halts,<br>And they may live to mend their parents’ faults. |
| WILLIAMSON | Why, [[Well\|well]] said, wife. I‘[[Faith\|faith]], thou cheer’st my heart.<br>Give me thy hand. Let’s kiss, and so let’s part.<br>He kisses her on the ladder |
| DOLL | The next kiss, Williamson, shall be in heaven.<br>Now cheerly, lads! George Betts, a hand with thee. |
| *(didascalia)* | To Clown Betts And thine too, Ralph. And thine, good |
| DOLL | honest Sherwin.<br>Now let me tell the women of this town<br>No stranger yet brought Doll to lying down.<br>So long as I an Englishman can see,<br>Nor French nor Dutch shall get a kiss of me.<br>And when that I am dead, for me yet say<br>I died in scorn to be a stranger’s prey. |
| *(didascalia)* | A great shout and noise within |
| VOICES WITHIN | Pardon, pardon, pardon, pardon!<br>Room for the Earl of Surreyl Room there, room! |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Surrey |
| SURREY | Save the man’s life, if it be possible! |
| SHERIFF | It is too late, my lord, he’s dead already. |
| SURREY | I tell ye, Master Sheriff, you are too forward<br>To make such haste with men unto their [[Death\|death]].<br>I think your pains will merit little thanks,<br>Since that his highness is so merciful<br>As not to spill the [[Blood\|blood]] of any subject. |
| SHERIFF | My noble lord, would we so much had known!<br>The Council’s warrant hastened our dispatch.<br>It had not else been done so suddenly. |
| SURREY | [[Sir Thomas More]] humbly upon his knee<br>Did beg the lives of all, since on his word<br>They did so gently yield. The [[King]] hath granted it,<br>And made him Lord High Chancellor of England,<br>According as he worthily deserves.<br>Since [[Lincoln]]’s life cannot be had again,<br>Then for the rest, from my dread sovereign’s lips,<br>I here pronounce free pardon for them all— |
| ALL | (flinging up caps)<br>God save the [[King]]! God save the [[King]],<br>My good Lord Chancellor and the Earl of Surreyl |
| DOLL | And Doll desires it from her very heart<br>More’s name may live for this right noble part;<br>And whensoe’er we talk of Ill May Day<br>Praise More, whose honest words our falls did stay. |
| SURREY | In [[Hope\|hope]] his highness’ clemency and [[Mercy\|mercy]],<br>Which in the arms of mild and meek compassion<br>Would rather clip you, as the loving [[Nurse\|nurse]]<br>Oft doth the wayward infant, than to leave you<br>To the sharp rod of justice; so to draw you<br>To shun such lewd assemblies as beget<br>Unlawful riots and such traitorous acts<br>That, striking with the hand of private hate,<br>Maim your dear country with a public wound.<br>O God, that [[Mercy\|mercy]], whose majestic brow<br><br>Should be unwrinkled, and that awe-full justice,<br><br>Which looketh through a veil of sufferance<br><br>Upon the frailty of the multitude,<br><br>Should with the clamours of outrageous wrongs<br><br>Be stirred and wakened thus to punishment!<br><br>But your deserved [[Death\|death]] he doth forgive.<br>Who gives you life, pray all he long may live. |
| ALL | God save the [[King]]! God save the [[King]],<br>My good Lord Chancellor, and the Earl of Surreyl |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt |
| ALL | [Original Text (Munday)] |
| *(didascalia)* | [Addition III (playhouse scribe; attributed to Shakespeare)] |
